# Source Register and Verification Record

Prepared 6 November 2026

## 1. Purpose

This file is the last of the 18 deliverables. It lists every source behind the research, shows how 46 key claims were re-checked against their sources, and records the corrections, source conflicts, planning assumptions and data gaps. Nothing here is new research: every entry comes from the ten research notes (A to J) and the two fact-check files (K and L).

---

## 2. How the research was done

| Round | Workstream | Content | Model | Web searches (as stated in the notes file) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A | Apollo company and farm business | Opus | About 23 (plus direct downloads of primary PDFs) |
| 1 | B | Farmer buying behaviour | Opus | 35 (allowance 40) |
| 1 | C | Competitors and the farm tyre market | Opus | 37 of the 40 allowed (the rest were direct page and PDF fetches) |
| 1 | D | Cricket assets and rural cricket | Sonnet | About 34 used of 35 allowed |
| 1 | E | News and policy 2025-26 | Sonnet | 29 of 30 allowed |
| 2 | F | Geography and distribution | Sonnet | About 34 of the 38 allowed |
| 2 | G | Trends to 2028 | Sonnet | 38 (full allowance) |
| 2 | H | Consulting benchmarks | Sonnet | 37 of 38 allowed |
| 2 | I | Costs and measurement methods | Sonnet | 37 of 38 allowed |
| 2 | J | Rural cricket audience and gap-filling | Sonnet | About 29 (plus direct fetches) |
| 3 | K | Fact-check: Apollo and cricket claims (22 claims) | Sonnet | not stated |
| 3 | L | Fact-check: farmer, market and cost claims (24 claims) | Sonnet | not stated |

Round 1 had five workstreams. Round 2 had five workstreams, all run by Sonnet. Round 3 had two independent fact-checkers, both Sonnet. Each fact-checker re-opened the sources behind its claims and gave a verdict.

The synthesis, diagnosis, strategy, budget, measurement and final recommendation (files 04, 05, 09 to 14, 16 and 00) were written by Fable from the notes. The research files 01, 02, 03, 06, 07, 08 and 15 were compiled from the notes by Opus and Sonnet under a fixed brief, and then corrected against the fact-check.

All research was desk research on public sources on 6 November 2026. No interviews, no company data and no primary survey were available.

---

## 3. Evidence labels used across the files

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| [Verified fact] | Confirmed in a primary source (company filing, official release, government data) or in two independent credible sources. |
| [Fact - single source] | Found in one credible source only. Not confirmed by a second source. |
| [Proxy evidence] | A related measure used because the direct measure does not exist (for example a single-district study, or a portal listing standing in for a dealer price). |
| [Unverified] | Seen only in press, blogs, vendor pages or search snippets, with no primary confirmation. Do not rely on it. |
| [Interpretation] | A reading of the evidence by the research team. Supported by facts but not itself a fact. |
| [Strategic hypothesis] | A proposed explanation or move that the pilot is designed to test. Not yet evidenced. |
| [Planning assumption] | A number chosen because no data exists, so that the budget and commercial model can run. Listed in section 8. |
| [Forecast] | A projection by a named forecaster (for example CRISIL or ICRA) or by management. It may not happen. |
| Data unavailable – requires primary research or company confirmation. | The standard gap sentence. It means the number was searched for and not found in public sources. |

Source-quality order used when sources disagreed (best first):

1. Company filings and official publications
2. Government data
3. Industry bodies and rating agencies
4. Consulting and academic work
5. Business press
6. Trade portals and vendor pages (lowest)

---

## 4. Fact-check results

### 4.1 Count of verdicts

Both fact-check files have a results table and a "Corrections needed" list. Neither has a summary count, so there was no disagreement between a summary and its table. The counts below are counted from the table rows.

| Fact-check file | Claims | Confirmed (plain) | Confirmed, with a caveat in the verdict | Confirmed with correction | Partly confirmed | Could not check |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K (Apollo and cricket) | 22 | 15 | 1 (K20: "CONFIRMED (published 4 Nov 2026); no farm/rural/cricket comment found") | 3 (K14, K15, K22) | 3 (K4, K13, K19) | 0 |
| L (farmers, market, costs) | 24 | 17 | 2 (L9 "with date caveat"; L24 "KCC via second source") | 0 | 4 (L15, L18, L20, L21) | 1 (L16) |
| Total | 46 | 32 | 3 | 3 | 7 | 1 |

In short: 38 claims were confirmed (35 with no correction to the claim, 3 with a correction), 7 were partly confirmed and 1 could not be checked. L15 carries the verdict "PARTLY CONFIRMED / CORRECTION" and is counted as partly confirmed.

## 5. Corrections made after the fact-check

Every correction the fact-checkers called for is below. The last column says "see correction pass" because the fact-check files do not name the deliverables affected.

**The correction pass.** On 6 November 2026, after both fact-checks, one pass applied these corrections to the deliverables. Files changed: 01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 13, 15 and 16. Files 11, 12 and 14 needed no change, and file 00 was written after the fact-check. A search of files 01 to 16 afterwards found no remaining instance of the superseded statements on the Q2 FY27 results, the call-centre registration line, the Rs 1,190 crore figure, the old Agriculture Census shares, or the "announcement pending" wording on Muthoot FinCorp. The raw notes in `research_notes\` were left as written, so they still carry the uncorrected statements.

| # | Item | What the files said before | What is correct | Files affected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Apollo Q2 FY27 results (K20) | Not yet published; due by about 14 Nov 2026 (notes A and E) | Published on 4 November 2026: revenue Rs 7,968 cr (+16.7%); net profit Rs 292 cr (+13.3%); operating margin about 11% (aggregator figure; Apollo's own release and call not opened). No farm, rural or cricket comment found | See correction pass |
| 2 | Muthoot FinCorp home title sponsorship (K15) | Press-reported only (25 Sep 2026); official announcement pending | Confirmed by an official BCCI release on 26 September 2026 (per Business Today). Scope: all international series hosted in India plus BCCI domestic competitions. Value Rs 130-150 cr (Rs 4.3-4.85 cr per match, about 35 matches); one outlet says up to Rs 171 cr (snippet only). Apollo remains lead (jersey) sponsor | See correction pass |
| 3 | Apollo's page on title rights (K14, K15) | Apollo holds title rights for home internationals and BCCI domestic tournaments (from Apollo's page) | Apollo's page lists them, but this collides with the Muthoot deal. Present Apollo as lead (jersey) sponsor; do not present home-title or domestic rights as held by Apollo until confirmed | See correction pass |
| 4 | Rs 579 cr contract value and bidders (K14) | Rs 579 cr stated as the contract value; third bidder sometimes "JK Tyre" | Rs 579 cr is a reported (Times of India) figure; BCCI has not disclosed it. Third bidder is JK Cement (CricTracker, Storyboard18); Campaign India says "JK Tyre" and should not be cited for bidders. Apollo's page says "Three years" and gives no value, match count or date | See correction pass |
| 5 | Warranty registration line (K13) | Farm tyre warranty is registered by calling the call centre within a month of purchase | The call-centre registration line is not on Apollo's farm warranty pages. It comes from a passenger-tyre page (snippet only). Farm pages give only the customer-care number 1800 212 7070 for complaints, with proof of purchase | See correction pass |
| 6 | CEAT warranty (K13) | CEAT farm tyres have a 7-year warranty | CEAT's 7 years applies to tractor tyres made on or after 1 January 2019 (5 years before). The CEAT page does not say "farm tyres" | See correction pass |
| 7 | Motilal Oswal "25% replacement" (K19) | About 25% of farm tyre demand is replacement, presented as a market fact | The sentence says "the farm category" and does not say whether it describes Apollo or the industry. Attribute it to the Motilal Oswal note of 27 Dec 2025 (written after meeting Apollo management). Do not present it as an industry statistic | See correction pass |
| 8 | Agriculture Census shares (L15) | Semi-medium 9.43%; medium 3.76% | Semi-medium 9.55%; medium 3.80% (calculated from the official PIB count table: 1,46,454 thousand holdings). Marginal 68.45%, small 17.62% and large 0.57% were right. Operated-area shares were not found in an official source | See correction pass |
| 9 | State average holding sizes (L16) | Punjab 3.62 ha, Rajasthan 2.73, Haryana 2.22, UP 0.73 stated as fact | Could not be checked; only a search summary of a Lok Sabha annexure supports them. Label unverified until checked at the annexure or agcensus.da.gov.in | See correction pass |
| 10 | PAU stall rates (L18) | Rs 34,000-75,000 for the September 2026 Kisan Mela | The range is right but excludes 18% GST, and the rate PDF carries no month or year. Drop "September 2026" or confirm from the PAU notice | See correction pass |
| 11 | Trade-programme figures: Castrol, Ambuja, Pidilite (L21) | Castrol about 1.5 lakh mechanics; Ambuja 70,000+; Fevicol about 40,000, as current | Castrol: over 1.5 lakh mechanics and retailers enrolled on Castrol Fast Scan (Sep 2019). Ambuja: over 70,000 contractors enrolled in Ambuja Abhimaan in 2021; 2.93 lakh by FY2025. Pidilite: about 40,000 applicators in close to 300 Fevicol Champions Clubs (undated interview; an older annual report said 45,000+) | See correction pass |
| 12 | Kisan Credit Card figure (L24) | KCC coverage 44.1% cited to ThePrint | ThePrint does not carry 44.1%. Cite Down To Earth | See correction pass |
| 13 | Farm revenue estimate (K3) | About Rs 1,190 cr (6% of Rs 19,816 cr) | Say "about Rs 1,200 cr (6%, rounded)". The true range is about Rs 1,090-1,290 cr because 6% is rounded | See correction pass |
| 14 | CRISIL tractor share (K4) | Tractor tyres 6% | Say "CRISIL (14 Aug 2026) puts tractor tyres at about 6% of domestic sales, in line with Apollo's 6% standalone farm share". The CRISIL sentence reads "tractor and light commercial segment 6% and 9%, respectively" (T&B 55%, PV 22%) | See correction pass |
| 15 | Annual report on H2 (K22) | Domestic farm replacement "grew strongly, especially in the second half" | "delivered strong growth", "supported by a recovery in demand during the second half" (FY26 annual report). H1 was weaker | See correction pass |
| 16 | 2019 "8-10%" claim (K12) | Shown beside today's 5-6% as a trend | A historic statement from 2019. Do not set side by side without explaining; the basis is unexplained | See correction pass |
| 17 | CEAT Rs 240 cr (K16) | CEAT IPL deal about Rs 240 cr | Rs 240 cr is for "certain segments"; its separate timeout partnership is also about Rs 240 cr over five years. Do not add or confuse them | See correction pass |
| 18 | Management quote on rural reach (K7) | Sponsorship will give rural reach for agri and trucks | Rural reach is tied first to car and two-wheeler tyres; "agri and truck" is the add-on. Exact date 14 Nov 2025 | See correction pass |
| 19 | First Sri Lanka ODI venue (K17) | Delhi, 13 Dec 2026 | Moved to Visakhapatnam per a related BCCI story. Do not quote Delhi | See correction pass |
| 20 | Tractor Gyan and FADA totals (L2, L3) | Used side by side | Different series (VAHAN-based 10,30,895 vs FADA 10,50,077 vs estimated wholesale 11,60,231). Do not mix. Maharashtra, Rajasthan and Gujarat shares are calculations; UP and Maharashtra are "joint top" (1 unit apart) | See correction pass |
| 21 | IFPRI figures (L5) | 5.81 million as tractors in use; about 6 million four-wheel tractors | 5.81 m is cumulative registrations to 2012 (MoRTH), not tractors in use. "About 6 million" is a crude estimate (CSAM/UNESCAP 2016). Punjab + Haryana = 17.8% together | See correction pass |
| 22 | J.D. Power dates and sources (L6, L7) | "2014 study" and 2018 index treated as trend evidence | The 2014 study is titled the "2015" inaugural study (fieldwork Jul-Nov 2014). The 6.5 per 100 refers to excessive tyre wear. 2017 data seen only via Tyrepress. The 2018 index is reported on MRF's own page and is the first, so no trend can be claimed | See correction pass |
| 23 | NSS advice shares (L8) | All figures labelled kharif 2018-19 | The 49% and 20% sentences are not labelled "kharif" on the page; the 23%, 3.1%, 1.3% and 0.3% are | See correction pass |
| 24 | Tractor-use district studies (L9) | Presented as typical usage | Old, small studies (2003, 2011, 2017). Label as older studies; do not generalise | See correction pass |
| 25 | Monsoon (L11) | 2026 monsoon "about 13% below normal" | Use "12.6% below the long-period average (87% of LPA)" and 2025 "107.9% of LPA" | See correction pass |
| 26 | Portal tyre prices (L12) | Prices stated as market prices | Portal listings, not dealer transaction prices; the two portals differ by Rs 160-400 per tyre | See correction pass |
| 27 | Ormax and Free Dish (L14) | 612 m, 678 m and 49 m used loosely | 612 m = cricket audience; 678 m = all sports; "audience" = watched 30+ minutes on TV or OTT in the past year. 49 m = households (FY2024-25 annual report), not viewers | See correction pass |
| 28 | McKinsey sponsorship figures (L20) | Quoted as McKinsey's own survey | McKinsey attributes the one-third-to-one-half figure to "industry research". Cite as "McKinsey (2014), citing industry research"; re-check wording before quoting | See correction pass |
| 29 | GST wording (L1) | One old rate for all "parts" | "Tyres and tubes 18% to 5%" only; one outlet gives 28% for HSN 8708 parts; road tractors over 1800cc excluded (18%) | See correction pass |

The "What the files said before" column is the fact-checkers' description of the claim as it stood in the notes and early drafts. It is not a re-read of the 17 other deliverables.

## 7. Conflicts between sources and how they were handled

| Topic | Source A says | Source B says | How the files treat it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Contract term | Apollo's page and BCCI wording: "3-year" / "Three years"; exchange4media agrees | Business Standard, Onmanorama, Campaign India: about 2.5 years, to March 2028. Angel One and Nenow say 2027 | Use "to March 2028" (majority of press; matches Khel Now and Storyboard18). Treat "3-year" as approximate wording |
| Title rights: Apollo's page vs Muthoot FinCorp | Apollo's page: title sponsor rights for all home internationals (men's and women's) and BCCI domestic tournaments, plus logo on front and leading arm | Muthoot FinCorp is home title sponsor to March 2028 (BCCI release of 26 Sep 2026, per Business Today) | Apollo is lead (jersey) sponsor. Home-title and domestic rights are not shown as held by Apollo until confirmed. Flagged as a conflict |
| Rs 579 cr contract value | Times of India and other outlets: about Rs 579 cr | BCCI has not disclosed any value | Shown as "reported, not official". Per-match figures vary: Rs 4.77 cr (headline), 4.5 cr (Angel One, Cricket Addictor), 4.08 cr (579/142) and 4.79 cr (579/121) by Khel Now arithmetic |
| Canva bid | CricTracker and most outlets: Rs 544 cr | Morning Context and Hindustan Times (snippet): Rs 554 cr | Use Rs 544 cr (majority). Third bidder is JK Cement (CricTracker, Storyboard18), not "JK Tyre" (Campaign India) |
| Q1 FY27 margin and raw materials | InvestyWise: revenue about Rs 7,400 cr; consolidated EBITDA margin 11.7%; raw materials +17%. Fact-check K gives Q1 FY27 revenue Rs 7,397.79 cr | Kotak Neo: total income Rs 7,456 cr; EBITDA Rs 950 cr (12.7% of total income). A call summary: basket up about 25%. An analyst note: materials 55.3% of revenue | Different bases and definitions. Margin and raw-material figures are labelled [Unverified]; not reconciled to the filing |
| FY27 capex | Management (15 May 2026): INR 35 bn (about Rs 3,500 cr), about 80% for growth and capacity | CRISIL: about Rs 3,000 cr in FY27 and about Rs 2,200 cr in FY28. A Q1 FY27 summary: above Rs 3,000 cr | Treated as [Forecast]. Management's Rs 3,500 cr is quoted, with the CRISIL figure noted |
| DD Free Dish homes | Prasar Bharati annual report FY2024-25, and FICCI-EY via exchange4media: about 49 m homes | FICCI-EY 2026 (search summary): Free TV 45 m (2023) to 53 m (2025). Impact on Net: 50-60 m; another table 60+ m | Use 49 m households as the main figure. 53 m is single-source; higher estimates are [Unverified] |
| Number of Custom Hiring Centres | Autocar Professional (Apr 2026): 75,915 CHCs, Punjab 11,148 (no underlying source named) | Search summaries: 27,554 CHCs + 646 hi-tech hubs + 25,608 farm machinery banks; a Lok Sabha-based 43,954 combined | Not used as one national number. Definitions probably differ (scheme-funded vs all). Marked [Unverified] |
| Tractor Gyan vs FADA vs wholesale totals | Tractor Gyan FY26 (VAHAN registrations): 10,30,895 | FADA retail: 10,50,077; TMA wholesale (estimated): 11,60,231 | Different counters. Each figure is shown with its series name; never mixed |
| Farm share of revenue | 2019 release: farm "nearly 8-10%" of Indian revenues; consolidated Farm/Off-Highway slide: 8%, 9%, 10% | Investor presentations: farm 5%, 5%, 6% of standalone revenue | Cannot be reconciled from public data (different denominator or real dilution). Both shown with their definitions, never as a trend |
| BKT's share claims | EVO India, 25 Feb 2026: BKT has "over 20 per cent" of the agricultural market | 2020: 7-8% of Indian off-highway tyres. Unsourced blogs: Apollo over 25% of tractor tyres and CEAT largest, or BKT leads off-highway | Company claims on different years and denominators. Treated as [Fact - single source] (company claim) or [Unverified]; no farm-tyre share table is built |
| Apollo warranty period | Apollo's own page: 7 years pro-rata from week of manufacture (July 2018 onward); a TractorGyan Q&A (26 Jul 2025) agrees | TractorGyan rear-tyre section and TractorKarvan listings (FX 515, Farmking 12.4-28): "2 years" | Use Apollo's page (7 years). The portal "2 years" is not used |
| Which league the Aditya Birla title deal belongs to | Note D: Maharashtra Premier League (MCA), Aditya Birla Group reported title sponsor 2026 (unverified single source) | Note J: Madhya Pradesh League (MPL T20) 2026, Aditya Birla Group title sponsor (Pulse); value not disclosed | Unresolved. Both notes use the abbreviation "MPL". Not used as a budget benchmark |
| Area served per regulated market | Lok Sabha AU2419 (via search summary): one market per about 406 sq km nationally; NCF norm 80 sq km | Dalwai model-law document: 485 sq km; a March 2014 summary: 457 sq km average (118 in Punjab to 11,214 in Meghalaya) | Single-source and indicative only. Punjab about 116 sq km per market is a computation |
| ODI World Cup 2027 start | Esquire and ICC (summary): tournament from 2 October 2027 | One search summary: 4 October start | Resolved by the fact-check: ICC published the schedule on 1 Oct 2026; 2 Oct is the Super Series start; the main group stage opens 7 Oct |
| Agriculture Census shares | Secondary table (IASpoint): semi-medium 9.43%, medium 3.76% | Official PIB counts: 9.55% and 3.80% | Official figures used. Operated-area shares are not quoted (unverified) |
| Monsoon 2026 | Fact-check: 12.6% deficit, 87% of LPA | Some outlets "13%"; UNI: "fourth weakest since 2001"; Business Standard: worst since 2015 | Use 12.6% / 87% of LPA; the ranking is not used |
| Raw material share of revenue | About 46.5% in Q3 FY25 (computed from reported figures) | 55.3% in Q1 FY27 (one analyst note, not verified against the filing) | 46.5% is used for the planning assumption; 55.3% is flagged [Unverified] |
| CEAT Rs 240 cr | IPL official partner deal (certain segments): about Rs 240 cr over five years | Its separate strategic-timeout partnership: also about Rs 240 cr over five years | Never added together or read as one total |
| Tractor hours per year | District studies: 784-856 hours a year | A blog (KhetiGaadi, no data source): 500-600 hours a year | District studies used as old proxy evidence; the blog is [Unverified] |

## 8. Planning assumptions used in the budget and commercial model (not facts)

| Assumption | Value | Why it had to be assumed |
|---|---|---|
| Tractors in use per programme tehsil | 5,000 / 8,000 / 12,000 | No district or tehsil stock data is public |
| Rear tyres replaced per tractor per year | 0.3 | No measured tyre life in Indian conditions; maker and portal claims of 1,500 to 4,000 hours |
| Front tyres replaced per tractor per year | 0.4 | As above |
| Apollo's realisation per tyre | Rs 14,000 rear, Rs 2,800 front | Derived from portal prices less 5% GST and an assumed 10% trade margin; dealer margins are not published |
| Contribution margin on extra sales | 25% / 30% / 35% | Apollo does not disclose it; raw materials were about 46.5% of revenue in Q3 FY25 |
| Points of the local replacement market won | 1.5 / 4 / 7 | No benchmark exists for a farmer programme's sales effect |
| Field cost per tehsil per year at scale | Rs 2.9 lakh engine, Rs 2.5 lakh cricket layer | Built from vendor estimates and working figures |
| Rural survey cost per interview | Rs 800 / 1,200 / 2,000 | No Indian rate card found |
| Intra-cluster correlation for brand recall | 0.05 | No published value for brand recall; a farm-advisory trial assumed 0.15 for yield |
| Pilot size | 60 tehsils in 12 districts, three arms of 20 | Design choice |
| Scale size | 200 tehsils | Planning figure, subject to the stock test and supply |
| Pilot budget | Rs 4.7 / 7.4 / 11.8 crore | The case brief states no budget |
| Programme names (Apollo XI, Lambi Paari, Apollo Kisan Cup, Scorecard) | Working titles | Not cleared with the BCCI or checked for trademark |

---

## 10. Full source register

The register below was extracted by script from the "Source register" table in each of the ten research notes (A to J). Each row is copied unchanged: numbering, titles, dates, "supports" text and reliability ratings, including cautions such as "snippet only" or "page not opened". The URL column in the notes is plain text; here it is shown as a link to the same address. The row count for each workstream was checked against the notes file after pasting.

| Workstream | Notes file | Sources in the notes file | Rows pasted below | Numbering used in the notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Apollo company and farm business | 35 | 35 | 1 to 35 |
| B | Farmer buying behaviour | 44 | 44 | 1 to 44 |
| C | Competitors and the farm tyre market | 44 | 44 | S1 to S44 |

## 11. Round 4 sources (6 November 2026)

These are the sources behind research notes N1 to N7, which were added in round 4. They were not in the first ten notes (A to J). Letters K and L are already used for the two fact-check files and are not reused. Letters M and P are skipped. The new groups are N, O, Q, R, S and T. None of these letters was used before, so no group was moved to a later letter. Each row is copied from the source list in the notes file. Each ID is the group letter plus the number the notes file gives the source. URLs are copied exactly; if a notes row has no URL, the cell says "no URL in notes". Rows marked "(blank in notes)" had an empty cell in the notes file.

| T-N7-3 | Indian farmers' optimism is driving interest in new products | McKinsey (Nitika Nathani, Pradeep Prabhala, Dwijo Goswami, Akshita Agarwal) | 15 January 2025 | [https://www.mckinsey.com/in/our-insights/indian-farmers-optimism-is-driving-interest-in-new-products](https://www.mckinsey.com/in/our-insights/indian-farmers-optimism-is-driving-interest-in-new-products) | Survey of 1,031 Indian farmers in early 2024; dealer-first model with digital in support; credit and interest rates | Verified fact: primary source read; notes do not say how many respondents own tractors |
| T-N7-4 | The granularity of growth | McKinsey | (blank in notes) | no URL in notes | Described generically in the re-analysis (growth comes mostly from where a company competes, at a fine-grained level) | Not opened in N7; Unverified as to exact wording |
| T-N7-5 | McKinsey pricing "pocket price waterfall" | McKinsey | (blank in notes) | no URL in notes | Described generically in the re-analysis (list price is eroded by discounts and terms before it reaches the seller's pocket) | Not opened in N7; Unverified as to exact wording |

Note N6 gives one source (Google Trends) and no separate source table. Note N7 has no source table either; the three opened McKinsey pages come from its source lines, and the two items under "Not opened" have no URL in the notes.

Rows per group: N = 33, O = 14, Q = 36, R = 62, S = 54, T = 6.

## 12. Round 5 fact-check (6 November 2026)

A third fact-check pass re-opened the sources behind 28 claims from the round 4 notes (N1 to N5). The full table, with what each source says, is in `research_notes\P_factcheck_round4.md`. The table below is the short version. The correction column says what changed in the McKinsey_Lens files. "None called for" means the notes list no correction for that claim; where the notes add a caveat, it is shown.

| # | Claim (short) | Verdict | Correction applied |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CRISIL tractor tyre demand FY19-FY24 (RHP 2024) | CONFIRMED (front/rear/trailer scope NOT stated for demand) | Cite the August 2024 prospectus; front, rear and trailer scope not stated |
| 2 | Farm/agri tyre exports 5,601k FY24; 5,766k FY23; imports 4.98k FY24 | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 3 | FY24 all-India tyre demand by category; tractor FY29 outlook | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 4 | Motilal Oswal on Apollo, 27 Dec 2025 | CONFIRMED, with context | Read as Apollo's own farm business, not an industry figure |
| 5 | FADA FY26 rural shares and units | CONFIRMED | None called for; shares are by registration office |
| 6 | Mahindra: installed tractor base 12.2 m by FY2030 (from 11 m) | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 7 | CRISIL (Mar 2026): agriculture 70-75% of tractor demand, construction/other 25-30% | NOT CONFIRMED at the stated URL / PARTLY CONFIRMED elsewhere | Re-dated to April 2025; "25 to 30%" dropped |
| 8 | CEAT Q4 FY26 FY26 standalone revenue mix | CONFIRMED | None called for; mix is standalone, Off Highway includes non-farm |
| 9 | Goodyear India FY2025-26 MD&A | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 10 | J.D. Power 2018 India TTSI | CONFIRMED | None called for; 18% and 22% are the lowest case |
| 11 | Kaur 2024, Punjab cotton belt tractor study | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 12 | Trelleborg 5,000-hour tread-wear guarantee | CONFIRMED (with wording fix on the examples) | Wording fixed: 200 unused hours earn 4%, 500 hours 10%; large sizes only |
| 13 | CRISIL on Apollo Tyres, 14 Aug 2026 | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 14 | CARE on Celite Tyre Corporation, 8 Mar 2022 | CONFIRMED, with a caveat | Say FY20, last disclosed |
| 15 | GST on retreaded tractor tyres 18% to 5% from 1 Nov 2026 | CONFIRMED (rate "from 18%" is inferred, not printed in the notification) | None called for; only retreaded tractor tyres move to 5% |
| 16 | Poonawalla Fincorp tyres on EMI | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 17 | Farm King prices (91trucks) and 1.5x life claim | CONFIRMED | None called for; portal price is undated |
| 18 | Apollo "Champions offer" 7 Dec 2025 - 31 Mar 2026 | PARTLY CONFIRMED (tyre categories differ from the implication) | Offer was for bike and car tyres, not farm tyres |
| 19 | Maruti rural share 3.5% (2007-08) to 8.5% (2008-09); 3,000+ rural sales executives | CONFIRMED WITH CAVEAT | Say 8.5% in 2008-09, as at March 2009 |
| 20 | Hero Honda rural ~45% of sales in 2011; over 1 lakh villages | CONFIRMED (date fix) | Article dated 2 June 2011; quote fixed |
| 21 | eDART-IPL25: tyre brands second most recalled category, about 1 in 3 | CONFIRMED | None called for; no sample size given |
| 22 | MRF-Gill bat sponsor from March 2025 (ex-CEAT); Tendulkar, Kohli bats; MRF Pace Foundation 1987 | PARTLY CONFIRMED | Re-sourced to Manifest, afaqs and Wikipedia |
| 23 | CEAT IPL strategic time-out partner since 2015; cricket ratings since 1995 | CONFIRMED (two sources) | None called for |
| 24 | Castrol Fast Scan: 1.5 lakh mechanics and retailers by 2019; payout per month | CONFIRMED (via Castrol's own copy; the Business Wire page is now empty) | Use Castrol's own copy; about Rs 6 crore a month |
| 25 | Tyre Trends 13 Feb 2026: Kanwar on shortages; CFO Rs 170 mn per tonne | CONFIRMED (reported speech; unit of "per tonne" not defined) | None called for; unit of "per tonne" is not defined |
| 26 | Apollo FY2026 annual report: farm replacement growth; new ag radial platform | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 27 | YouTube: Tractor Bajar (~6 lakh views); Vikas ki videos (~16 lakh views) | CONFIRMED | None called for |
| 28 | FTP 2027-31 not final as of 23 Sep 2026; England Test series in India early 2028 | CONFIRMED | None called for; status is as at 23 September 2026 |

**Verdict count, as the notes file states it:** 22 confirmed (of which four, rows 4, 14, 15 and 24, carry caveats); 3 confirmed with correction (rows 12, 19 and 20); 2 partly confirmed (rows 18 and 22); 1 not confirmed (row 7); 0 could not be checked.

Rows 6, 9, 13 and 16 were confirmed through a summarising fetch tool, not raw text. The notes flag this.

**Corrections applied to the McKinsey_Lens files**

- CRISIL's 70 to 75% agriculture share of tractor demand is re-dated to April 2025 and the "25 to 30%" is dropped (M04).
- Apollo's "Champions offer" (7 December 2025 to 31 March 2026) was for motorcycle and car tyre buyers, not farm tyres (M06).
- MRF cricket facts re-sourced: Gill signed March 2025 (Manifest); Kohli's bat since 2014 and Tendulkar's partnership of over a decade (afaqs); Pace Foundation 1987 (Wikipedia) (M05, M06).
- Trelleborg wording: 200 unused hours earn a 4% credit, 500 hours 10%; eligible sizes are for large tractors (M05).
- Motilal Oswal's 25% reads as Apollo's own farm business, from a meeting with management; it is not an industry figure (M02).
- Celite Tyre's 6.99% margin is FY20, the last year disclosed (M05).
- Castrol reported about Rs 6 crore a month reimbursed to 1.5 lakh users in September 2019 (M05).
- One error found by the main session, not by the fact-checker: note N1 computed capital cost per tonne of yearly capacity as about Rs 46,500; the correct arithmetic from the same inputs (Rs 5,810 crore for about 342 tonnes a day, about 1.25 lakh tonnes a year) is about Rs 4.65 lakh. M02 uses the corrected figure. The article does not define "per tonne"; tonnes per day is inferred from the plant's size.

## 13. Planning assumptions added in the McKinsey-lens re-analysis

These are [Planning assumption] items, not facts. They add to the list in section 8.

| # | Assumption | Value | Where used | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Replacement market at manufacturers' prices | Rs 3,300 crore (range Rs 2,500 to 4,000 crore) | M02, M07 | CRISIL units x portal prices less 5% GST and a 10% trade margin |
| 2 | Manufacturer's realisation | Rs 14,200 rear; Rs 2,860 front; average about Rs 8,500 at a half-and-half mix | M02, M05, M07 | Portal prices; mix assumed |
| 3 | Working tractors | 8.5 million (7 to 11 million) | M02, M03, M07 | Mahindra's installed base, registrations scaled by Haryana's in-use ratio, NFHS-5 |
| 4 | Replacement spend per working tractor | About Rs 3,900 a year; about 0.5 tyres a year | M02, M03, M07 | Computed from rows 1 and 3 |
| 5 | Apollo's replacement share | About 9% (7 to 12%) | M02, M07 | 25% of about Rs 1,189 crore over row 1; rests on one broker note |
| 6 | Contribution margin before the programme | 30% | M02, M05, M07 | Raw materials at 55% of revenue plus 12 to 15% other variable cost |
| 7 | Cost of the offer | Rs 1,255 a rear tyre (mechanic Rs 300, dealer bonus Rs 400, credit Rs 225, guarantee reserve Rs 280, registration Rs 50); Rs 50 a front tyre; net contribution 22% | M05, M07 | Our design |
| 8 | Guarantee | 5% of tyres claim at an average credit of 25%; reserve 2% of covered sales; about Rs 125 crore of existing sales covered | M05, M07 | No maker publishes a claim rate |
| 9 | Tyre weights | Rear about 62 kg (one retailer listing); front about 12 kg (assumed); average 37 kg | M02, M08 | Low reliability |
| 10 | Capital cost of new capacity | About Rs 4.65 lakh per tonne of yearly capacity | M02, M08 | Computed from the CFO's figure; unit inferred |
| 11 | Reference areas | Tehsil of 5,000 working tractors; district of 25,000 | M03, M07 | Haryana district counts |
| 12 | Field engine | Rs 1.31 lakh a tehsil a year fixed; Rs 2.06 lakh at full cost | M07, M08 | File 13 section 6, with changes |
| 13 | Cricket layer | Rs 1.20 lakh a tehsil a year; Rs 1.30 lakh at full cost | M07, M08 | File 13 section 6, with changes |
| 14 | National offer | Rs 7.0 crore a year plus Rs 1.0 crore one-time; about 2,000 dealerships assumed to stock farm tyres | M07 | Our design |
| 15 | Spending | FY27 Rs 2.8 crore; FY28 Rs 8.3 crore; FY29 Rs 7.7, 12.2 or 15.1 crore by what passes the gates | M07 | Built from rows 12 to 14 and file 13 unit costs |
| 16 | Share gains by case | FY28 average 0.2 / 0.5 / 1.0 points; FY29 average 1.0 / 2.5 / 4.0 points (low / base / high) | M07 | Assumptions, not forecasts; no analogue gives a benchmark |
| 17 | Test design | 30 districts, about 116 tehsils, four arms, 40 owners a tehsil, three waves, Rs 1,200 an interview; intra-cluster correlation 0.05; standard deviation of yearly change in tehsil sales 25% (40% as the cautious case) | M08 | Our design |
| 18 | Gates | Offer 2 points; field engine 5 points; cricket layer 3 points of local replacement share | M08 | Higher of full-cost break-even and detectable difference |
| 19 | Farm's share of the jersey fee | About Rs 14 crore a year (6% of about Rs 230 crore) | M06, M07 | Reported fee; allocation by revenue share |
| 20 | First-wave districts | 30 districts in three belts, listed in M03 section 6 | M03, M08 | Official counts where they exist; registrations elsewhere |

Row numbers in the "Basis" column ("rows 1 and 3", "rows 12 to 14") refer to this table.
